<!--
Hi everyone! I’m Josh – I’m a full time independent open source maintainer. I focus on static analysis tooling around JavaScript & TypeScript – most notably, typescript-eslint, the tooling that allows you to run ESLint and Prettier on TypeScript code.
I’m also the author of the recent Learning TypeScript book, published by O’Reilly.
Now, I’d heard of AI around when a lot of other devs did, which is to say, when image generation prompts got popular. Please enjoy a self-portrait of, and I’m quoting the prompt, …
-->

-->

<!--
Throughout this presentation, I’m going to push back against the idea of using AIs for development on their own, but I want to make two things clear from the beginning.
One, I am not an AI hater. This talk is not going to tell you to stop using AI. I only want to express the pros and cons of using it compared to other tools.
You can find links to the actual AI prompts I used in the slide annotations, available on my website.
-->

<!--
Fun fact, many developers go through a variant of the Gartner hype cycle after they first master TypeScript types.
In the peak of inflated expectations, they feel great about themselves -- as they should! They mastered the type system; all sorts of wild and wacky features like generics and conditional types are at their disposal! TypeScript!!
But, those wild and wacky types come at a cost. They’re harder to write, harder to read, and much harder to debug. Turns out many of the generics and conditional types they used at the peak were unnecessary and over-inflated code complexity. Thus we exit the Peak of Inflated Types and enter the Trough of Type Disillusionment.
But, fear not: we all learn from our mistakes. That’s what it means to be a developer. Having learned the hard way not to overuse TypeScript’s type system features, developers enter the third phase in their TypeScript journey: the Plateau of Productivity. It’s here that TypeScript shines the best.
-->

</div>

<!--
What we call AI is not intelligent. it's not AI. That's marketing.The current slate of AIs are advanced auto completes. The most rich, enhanced, beautifully crafted auto completes the human race has ever seen. But they're not intelligent and they don't know the context behind your requests.
This is not a pessimistic talk. AI is the latest of exponentially increasing productivity boosts. It's already making previously difficult to impossible tasks straightforward, and is only going to get better over time.But like all of innovations, AI naturally follows Gartner Hype Cycle. In this talk I want everyone to walk away understanding what AI still isn’t great at, and how to use existing ecosystem tools to fill in the gaps of this very promising tool.
-->

Issues asking for docs to be added are not the same as help requests.
-->

Type Checkers 🧠</span></p></div></div>

<!--
Static analysis is tooling that judges your code without running it. We’ve typically consolidated into three types on a spectrum from fast to powerful.
The fastest is formatters such as Prettier. They run very quickly on your code and don’t touch program logic – they just make the code look pretty. More on that later.
The most powerful are type checkers such as TypeScript. They run more slowly and build up a full understanding of your entire application. More on that later.
In the middle are linters such as ESLint. They typically run on a file-by-file basis, which makes them faster than type checkers. But they still work with program logic, which makes them slower than formatters. More on that later.
-->

<!--
Honestly, these are pretty reasonable tests! I’m omitting the test bodies of the second and third tests because they’re similar to the first. All three work well and pass on my machine. Hooray!
But, I do have some gripes here. First off, I’m a big fan of AAA testing: having each test explicitly Arrange, Act, and Assert on a single test case. But these test blocks have several cases grouped inside each test.
Second, there aren’t that many edge cases being tested: just empty arrays and no elements being divisible. But surely there are other edge cases we should test: for example, what about negative numbers?
When it comes to LLMs, you get out what you put in.
If you ask a very high-level, vague question, it’ll give you a high-level, vague response. It can’t know how specific a response you’re looking for unless you tell it.
-->

-->

<!--
In other words, what we’d really want here is not just passing TypeScript types, but also detect any discrepancies between the response and those types.
If there are discrepancies, reported as type errors, then we’d want to report those type errors back to the AI to have it correct the response data shape.
We can keep doing this until it satisfies our types.
-->

layout: none
transition: { name: fade, duration: 500 }
---

-->

<div class="pptx-group" data-shape="11"><div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:50.73px;left:753.43px;top:221.01px;width:34.46px;justify-content:flex-end;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:36px;line-height:43.2px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#FFCCCC;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:36px">😊</span></p></div><div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:41.07px;left:618.2px;top:179.59px;width:304.93px;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:28px;font-weight:500;line-height:33.6px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:28px;font-weight:500">Plateau of Productivity</span></p></div></div>
<svg class="pptx-shape" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="26.06 154.01 918.01 367.58" style="height:367.58px;left:26.06px;top:154.01px;width:918.01px"><path d="M72 475C135.3 398.3 173 217.9 235.9 201.1C298.8 184.3 379.8 360 449.5 374.2C519.2 388.4 576.2 303.4 654.3 286.6C732.4 269.7 820.3 276.7 898 274.2" fill="none" stroke="rgb(200,215,250)" stroke-width="9.5" /></svg>
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:84.65px;left:0px;top:1.44px;width:960px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:600;line-height:45.36px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:600">The Gartner Hype Cycle</span></p></div>
</div>

<!--
First, remember that AI is still very early stage. It’s in an early peak of inflated expectations, wherein we’re going to learn the hard way how best to use it.
-->

---

layout: none
transition: { name: fade, duration: 500 }
---

<div class="pptx" style="">
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:148.86px;left:101.71px;top:195.57px;width:756.57px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700;line-height:42px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700">As an open source maintainer,</span></p><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:700;line-height:50.4px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:700">I am begging you,</span></p><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:49px;font-weight:700;line-height:58.8px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:49px;font-weight:700">read the docs first.</span></p></div>
</div>

<!--
I’m personally already in the trough of disillusionment because of how many developers I see jump to AI before trying first-party documentation. Please, docs are important – try them first, whenever feasible.
-->

---

layout: none
transition: { name: fade, duration: 500 }
---

<div class="pptx" style="">
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:252px;left:0px;top:144px;width:960px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700;line-height:42px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700">Use </span><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:700">linting</span><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700">, </span><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:700">testing</span><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700">, and </span><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:700">type checking</span><br><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:700">to detect bad practices and likely bugs.</span></p><p style="font-size:35px;line-height:42px;text-align:center"><br></p><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700;line-height:42px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700">Especially in code generated for you.</span></p></div>
</div>

<!--
When writing code with AI, don’t forget to validate your code with existing tools such as linters and type checkers. You shouldn’t trust code written by anybody or anything, let alone a glorified autocomplete.
-->

---

layout: none
transition: { name: fade, duration: 350 }
---

<div class="pptx" style="">
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:148.86px;left:101.71px;top:195.57px;width:756.57px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700;line-height:42px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:35px;font-weight:700">LLM “AI” is the </span><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:700">smartest stupid</span><br><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'League Spartan';font-size:42px;font-weight:700">humanity has ever created.</span></p></div>
</div>

<!--
This talk isn’t meant to shoo you away from AI. It’s an incredible new tool and are already rapidly pushing the boundaries of what technology is able to do.
Just, remember that they’re one tool among many. There are tasks they’re good at, tasks they’re bad at, and ways they should be used to avoid mishaps.
I look forward to seeing what you all create using “AI” in conjunction with existing dev tools.
-->

---

layout: none
---
